PLANNING & DEVELOPMENT BOARD AGENDA
The regular meeting of the PLANNING & DEVELOPMENT BOARD will be held at 6:00 p.m. on TUESDAY, MARCH 24, 2026 in the
Common Council Chambers on the third floor of City Hall, 108 E Green Street. The meeting will also be streamed on the
City of Ithaca YouTube Channel at https://www.youtube.com/channel/UC7RtJN1P_RFaFW2IVCnTrDg.
Instructions for commenting to the Planning Board
Scheduled Public Hearings (Refer to the agenda for projects that have scheduled public hearings.)
There are two options to participate in a Public Hearing:
1. Submit comments by email no later than 12 p.m. on the day of the meeting (contacts below). Each comment is
limited to three minutes. Indicate in your email that the comment is for a public hearing.
2. Attend the meeting via Zoom and speak directly to the Board. Comments are limited to three minutes.
General Public Comments
Public comments are heard at the beginning of the meeting via Zoom. Written comments should be sent to the contact(s)
listed below. All comments received will be forwarded to the Planning Board for their consideration. Written comments
received in advance of the meeting give the Board/Committee time to consider them fully. If you want your comment read
aloud, please state so in your email and limit the comment to three minutes.
Please use the following contacts to submit comments or request access to the Zoom meeting: Planning Board Staff at
Pbstaff@cityofithaca.org or call 607-274-6550.
Start Times: Start times are approximate only — APPLICANTS are responsible for being available at whatever time(s) their agenda item(s) is actually discussed.
AGENDA ITEM Approx. Start Time
1) Agenda Review 6:00
2) Approval of Minutes – February 24, 2026 Minutes 6:05
3) Public Comments (See instructions above.) 6:10
4) Board Response to Public Comment 6:15
5) Advice of Counsel 6:20
6) Site Plan Review
A. Project: The Hive Presentation 6:40
Location: 132 Cherry Street Board Q&A 6:45
Applicant: 132 Cherry Street Clinton LLC Discussion 6:50
Actions: Preliminary and Final Modified Site Plan Approval
Project Description: The Planning Board granted preliminary and final approval of this project on
September 27, 2022 and granted an extension for site plan approval on September 26, 2024. The applicant

now proposes changes to the site plan due to newly adopted FEMA floodplain and regulatory floodway
maps. The changes include but are not limited to moving the location of both buildings, changing outdoor
amenities, and changing the unit count and square footage of the project. The project is located in the
Cherry Street District Zoning District and will require variance(s). This has been determined to be a Type 1
Action under the City of Ithaca Environmental Quality Review Ordinance §176-4 B(1) (h)[2], (k), and (n) and
the State Environmental Quality Review Act (“SEQRA”) §617.4 (b)(11) for which the Planning Board, acting
as Lead Agency, issued a Negative Declaration of Environmental Significance on August 23, 2022.
Project materials are available for download from the City website and are updated regularly:
https://www.cityofithaca.org/DocumentCenter/Index/1165
B. Project: 309 College Avenue Apartments Presentation 7:00
Location: 309 College Avenue Board Q&A 7:05
Applicant: Nick Robertson Discussion 7:10
Actions: CEQR Review- Transportation /Construction/Noise, Odor & Light
Project Description: The applicant proposes to demolish an existing 1-story fire station and construct a
new 8-story 55,000 SF mixed-use apartment building with 77 units and ground story 1500 SF retail space.
The project is located in the MU2 and will not require any variances. It is subject to Collegetown Design
Guidelines. This has been determined to be a Type 1 Action under the City of Ithaca Environmental Quality
Review Ordinance §176-4 B(1)(k) & (l), and the State Environmental Quality Review Act (“SEQRA”) §617.4
(11) and is subject to environmental review.
Project materials are available for download from the City website and are updated regularly:
https://www.cityofithaca.org/DocumentCenter/Index/1966
C. Project: Finger Lakes Reuse Site Improvements Presentation 7:20
Location: 214 Elmira Road Board Q&A 7:25
Applicant: Diane Cohen CEO, Finger Lakes ReUse, Inc. Discussion 7:30
Actions:  Preliminary & Final Site Plan Approval
Project Description: The applicant proposes making site improvements and installing two 600 SF awnings
to the east and west sides of the existing buildings. The project site improvements will disturb
approximately 29,185 SF (.67 acres) of the 2.37-acre site and include removing paving, improving
drainage, formalizing parking and loading areas, and adding green space. The applicants propose adding
22 parking spaces to the site for a total of 67 proposed spaces. The project is located in the B-5 zoning
district and is expected to not require any variances. This is an Unlisted Action under the City of Ithaca
Environmental Quality Review Ordinance §176 and the State Environmental Quality Review Act (“SEQRA”)
§617 and is subject to environmental review.
Project materials are available for download from the City website and are updated regularly:
https://www.cityofithaca.org/DocumentCenter/Index/651

8) Old/New Business 7:45
A. Board Policy Discussion-
The Ithaca Planning and Development Board has identified several policy issues of general significance. In
the interest of informing development policy overall as well as specific efforts like the ongoing rewrite of
the zoning code and expansion of Planned Unit Development Overlay Districts, the Planning Board will begin
a series of discussions to inform policy recommendations on these issues: Alignment of housing
development with identified needs and priorities; Funding and management options for affordable housing;
Planned Unit Development; Preservation of green space; Enhancement of sustainable design practices,
Discussion items this month include:
1. Zoning Advisory Committee- report on ongoing zoning rewrite deliberations
(Petrina, Rollman)
2. Prioritization of policy issues and discussion process

the parcel. We will need to formalize a maintenance and access easement,
typically(cid:3)(cid:1005)(cid:1004)(cid:332)(cid:3)(cid:381)n either side of the pipe. Add the exis(cid:415)ng water and sewer mains
to your Site Plan and U(cid:415)(cid:367)ity drawings.
c. Project will disturb >1ac and will require a Full SWPPP. Submit
to stormwater@cityofithaca.org for review.
d. Exis(cid:415)(cid:374)(cid:336)(cid:3)(cid:400)tormpipe under new proposed driveway will need to be replaced due to
proposed traffic load with HDPE pipe.

proposal

a. Water and Sewer comments sent via OpenGov
b. Street Permit will be required when working within the City ROW.
c. Connect both se(cid:272)(cid:415)ons of compost sock or silt fence due to asphalt removal areas

a. The exis(cid:415)ng sanitary sewer service to 309 College Ave is 4" Cast Iron. Can not
reduce pipe size from 6" leaving the building to a 4" lateral. A new sanitary sewer
service will be required.

lid to be <2% cross-slope.
c. Show proposed sidewalk topo and running slopes at trans(cid:349)(cid:415)(cid:381)(cid:374)(cid:400). Running
sidewalk slope should not be steeper than exis(cid:415)(cid:374)g curbline. Curb height should
be full reveal 6”, as shown.
d. Show a proposed construc(cid:415)on staging plan and work zone traffic control plan
that includes a pedestrian detour/sidewalk closure.

and 12” subbase.



PROPOSED RESOLUTION City of Ithaca Planning & Development Board
Modifications to 9/27/2022 Site Plan Approval The Hive
& Extension 9/26/2024 132 Cherry St
Modified Site Plan Approval
March 24, 2026
WHEREAS the City of Ithaca Planning and Development Board approved two 5-story, 143-unit apartment
mixed use buildings with a combined total gross floor area of 171,800 SF by Cherry Clinton, LLC on
September 27, 2022, and the site plan approval was extended on September 26, 2024, and
WHEREAS: the applicant now seeks modified site plan approval for the project with changes. The
applicant now proposes changes to the site plan due to newly adopted FEMA floodplain and regulatory
floodway maps. The changes include but are not limited to moving the location of both buildings,
changing outdoor amenities, and changing the unit count and square footage of the project. The project is
located in the Cherry Street District Zoning District and will require variance(s), and
WHEREAS: the applicant now proposes two 5-story buildings, with a combined total gross floor area of
160,800 SF. The buildings contain 148 residential units on four floors, three commercial spaces totaling
approximately 4,200, 40 +/- parking spaces on the ground floor, and indoor amenities including a fitness
room, community room with kitchen, dog wash, indoor bicycle storage, and secured package rooms.
Outdoor amenities include a picnic area, a waterfront courtyard, a plaza along Cherry Street, streetscape
improvements and landscaping. The project is located in in the Cherry Street District Zoning District and
will require two variances, and
WHEREAS: this is a Type 1 Action under the City of Ithaca Environmental Quality Review Ordinance
§176-4 B.(1)(k), (l), and (n), and the State Environmental Quality Review Act (“SEQRA”) §617.4 b. (11)
and is subject to environmental review, and
WHEREAS: the NYS Department of Environmental Conservation and the Tompkins County Department
of Health, both potentially involved agencies in this action, have consented to the Planning Board acting
as Lead Agency for this project, and
WHEREAS: the City of Ithaca Planning and Development Board, being the local agency, which has primary
responsibility for approving and funding or carrying out the action, did on April 26, 2022 declare itself Lead
Agency in Environmental Review for the project, and
WHEREAS: the Planning Board, acting as Lead Agency in Environmental Review, did on August 23,
2022, review and accept as adequate: a Full Environmental Assessment Form (FEAF), Part 1, submitted
by the applicant, and Parts 2 and 3 prepared by Planning staff; drawings titled Waterfront Rendering,
Courtyard Renderings, Rendering from Waterfront Trail Extension all dated 08/16/22 and all prepared by
CJS Architects; Landscape Plan L100 dated 07/29/22 and prepared by CJS Architects and RPK
Landscape Architecture, P.C.; Site Plan C3, Grading Plan C4, Utility Plan C5, all dated 07/29/22 and
prepared by Fagan Engineers & Land Surveyors PC; Ground Floor/Site Plan A-100 dated 07/29/22 and
prepared by CJS Architects; and other application materials, and
WHEREAS: interested parties have been given the opportunity to comment on the proposed project, and
any received comments have been considered, and
WHEREAS: legal notice was published and property posted in accordance with Chapters 276-6 B. (4)
and 176-12 A. (2) (c) of the City of Ithaca Code, and

WHEREAS: the Planning and Development Board held the required public hearing beginning on
February 24, 2026, and
WHEREAS: the Planning Board did, on August 23, 2022 determine, as elaborated in the FEAF Part 3,
that the proposed project will result in no significant adverse impacts on the environment and issued a
Negative Declaration of Environmental Significance, and
WHEREAS: in accordance with §176-7 E. of CEQR and §617.7(e) of SEQRA, the City of Ithaca
Planning and Development Board acting as Lead Agency has determined that (1) new information has
been discovered and (2) a change in circumstances related to the project has arisen that was not
previously considered, and the Lead Agency has determined that no significant adverse impact will occur,
and
WHEREAS: the Board, acting as Lead Agency in environmental review, has on February 24, 2026
reviewed and accepted as adequate the new information consisting of: a revised Full Environmental
Assessment Form (FEAF), Part1, submitted by the applicant and Parts 2 and 3, prepared by Planning
staff; the following revised drawings: Boring Location S1 dated 02/6/2020 and prepared by Elwyn &
Palmer Consulting Engineers, PLLC; Project Vicinity Map prepared by Fagan Engineers; Construction
Packet “The Hive” C-001-C13 (pp. 12) submitted February 18, 2026 and prepared by CJS Architects and
Fagan Engineers: Landscape Plan C-007, Tree Details C-011, Ground Floor Key Plan A-110 (1) all
submitted February 18, 2026 and prepared by CJS Architects; South Building Elevations A-201 (1) & A-
202 (1), North Building Elevations A-203 (1) & A-204 (1) all dated 1/21/2026 and prepared by CJS
Architects; and other supporting materials, and
WHEREAS: the Planning Board did, on February 24, 2026 determine, as elaborated in the FEAF Part 3,
that the proposed project will result in no significant adverse impacts on the environment and issued an
Amended Negative Declaration of Environmental Significance, and
WHEREAS: the Board of Zoning Appeals granted front yard and off-street loading variances conditioned
on the applicants providing at least 425 SF of loading space and on the establishment and maintenance of
a trail along the inlet on March 3, 2026, and
WHEREAS: this Board did on March 24, 2026 review and accept as adequate the following new and
revised drawing: Landscape Plan C-007 dated 02/26/26 and prepared by CJS Architects; and other
supporting materials, now, therefore, be it
RESOLVED: that the Planning and Development Board does hereby grant Modified Preliminary & Final
Site Plan Approval subject to the following:
The following conditions must be satisfied before issuance a Building Permit:
i. Plans, drawings and/or visualizations showing all proposed exterior mechanicals and associated
equipment including heat pumps, ventilation, etc., including appropriate screening if necessary
ii. Documentation from Ithaca Fire Department emergency access issues have been satisfied
iii. This site plan approval does not preclude any other permit that is required by City Code, such as
sign permits, tree permits, street permits
iv. Acceptance of the SWPPP by the City Stormwater Management Officer
The following conditions must be satisfied within six months of approval or the start of construction,
whichever comes first:

v. Submission of detailed planting plan including soil replacement specifications for tree planting
areas, planting schedule with at least one large shade tree, and plan to remove invasive plant
species
vi. Submission to the Planning Board for review and approval of placement, design, and
photometrics of site lighting fixtures
vii. Submission to the Planning Board for review and approval of all site details including but not
limited to landscaping details, exterior furnishings, walls, railings, bollards, paving, signage,
lighting, etc.
viii. Final approval of streetscape along Cherry Street in front of project site by the City Engineering
Department
The following conditions must be satisfied during the construction period:
ix. If impacted groundwater and/or soil are encountered during construction, it is required to be
handled in accordance with the applicable NYSDEC regulations and requirements and with the
involvement of both the City of Ithaca and the NYSDEC in the approval and monitoring of the
treatment system(s)
x. Noise producing construction activities will be limited to the hours between 7:30 A.M. and 5:30
P.M., Monday through Friday (or Saturday 9:00 A.M. to 5:30 P.M. with advance notification to
and approval by the Director of Planning and Development)
The following conditions must be satisfied before issuance of a Certificate of Occupancy:
xi. Any changes to the approved site plans must be submitted to Planning staff for review and may
require Board approval
xii. Installation of bike racks and/or bike storage
xiii. Installation of any required or proposed sidewalk to the satisfaction of the City Sidewalk
Coordinator
xiv. Repair, replacement or reconstruction of any City property damaged or removed during
construction including, but not limited to paving, sidewalk, curbing, trees or tree lawn, signage,
drainage structures, etc.
xv. Submission of any required executed easement, licenses or other legal agreements involving City
property
xvi. Installation of the BZA conditions for the granting of the use variances which include providing
at least 425 SF of loading space and on the establishment and maintenance of a trail along the
inlet
Moved by:
Seconded by:
In favor:
Against:
Abstain:
Absent:
Vacancies: One

City of Ithaca
DRAFT FULL ENVIRONMENTAL ASSESSMENT FORM – Part III
Project Name: 309 College Avenue Redevelopment (77 Units)
Date Created: 02/17/2026 Updated 03/17/2026
PROJECT DESCRIPTION
The applicant proposes to demolish an existing 1-story fire station and constructa new 8-story 55,000 SF
mixed-use apartment building with 77 units and ground story 1500 SF retail space. The project is located
in the MU-2 and will not require any variances. It is subject to Collegetown Design Guidelines.
This has been determined to be a Type 1 Action under the City of Ithaca Environmental Quality Review
Ordinance §176-4 B(1)(k) & (l), and the State Environmental Quality Review Act (“SEQRA”) §617.4 (11)
and is subject to environmental review.
IMPACT ON LAND
Provide Vibration monitoring plan
Provide Erosion Control PlanDetails
Provide GeoTech or similar documentation
The project site is locatedonanapproximately 0.1969 acres (8,576.9 SF) parcel located on College Avenue. The
parcel has been previously developed as in an urbanized area. Currently an existing 1-story abandoned fire
station is located on the site. Project implementation will require the demolition of the existing buildingand
parking lotat 215 College Avenue. Construction is expected to last 21months.According to memo submitted
by the applicant dated August 18, 2025
Insert vibration monitoring information.
The applicant proposes to build a new 8-story apartment building with a total finished area of approximately
55,000 SF. The applicant proposes 77 residential units with a unit mix ranging from studio to 3-bedroom
apartments for a totalof 112 beds, 3,250 SF of amenity space and approximately 1,500 SF of retail space on
first floor.
According to the FEAF Part 1, the applicant estimates approximately 1,400 CY of material will be excavated
from the project site. A Geotechnical Engineering Report prepared by….John P Stopen Engineering, LLP
The Lead Agency has determined that based on the information above, no significant impact to land is
anticipated.
IMPACT ON GEOLOGIC FEATURES
The site is in an urbanized area with no unique geologic features present, therefore the Lead Agency has
determined thatno significant impact on geologic features is anticipated.
IMPACT ON SURFACE WATER
There are no surface water features on or adjacentto the project site. The project sitegenerallydrainstowards
the west.

IMPACT ON GROUNDWATER
Provide GeoTech or other document describing depths to groundwater
The proposal is a housing project in an urbanized area and as such does not include operational activities
that impact groundwater.
According to aGeotechnical Engineering Report prepared by
Any groundwater encountered during excavation will be handled in accordance with all state and local
laws.
The Lead Agency has determined that based on the information aboveand with complete adherence to
state and local laws, no significant impact to groundwater is anticipated.
IMPACT ON FLOODING
The project site is not located in aflood zone, and itis not near any waterbody that may contribute to
flooding therefore, the LeadAgency has determined that no significant impact on flooding is
anticipated.
IMPACT ON AIR
According to information provided by the applicant, construction is projected to last approximately23
months. During construction, generators may be required to provide power to the site. Excavation and
preparation of foundations additionally create the potential for increased airborne dust and dirt
particles. Impacts to air quality will be limited to the period associated with construction activities.
During construction, the applicant will employ the following applicable dust control measures, as
appropriate:
(cid:120) Misting or fog spraying the site to minimize dust;
(cid:120) Maintaining crushed stone tracking pads at all entrances to the construction site;
(cid:120) Re-seeding disturbed areas to minimize bare exposed soils;
(cid:120) Keeping roads clear of dust and debris;
(cid:120) Requiring construction trucks to be covered; and

The Lead Agency has determined that with the mitigation measures during construction identified
above, no significant impact to surface wateris anticipated.
IMPACT ON PLANTS AND ANIMALS
Provide tree protection detailsand sapling species
The project site is in an urbanized area and was previously developed. Wildlife likely to be encountered
on or near the project site include invertebrates, small mammals, and birds. Project development
requires the removal of all existing vegetation except forone sapling located on College Avenue which
will remain.
The applicants propose a number of smaller plantings including shrubbery, evergreens and groundcover
perennials. (Site Layout & Planting Plan L101, prepared by QPK Design 02/18/2026).A final landscaping
planwill be reviewed and approved during the site plan review process.
The Lead Agency has determined that based on the information above, no significant impact to plants
and animalsis anticipated.
IMPACT ON AGRICULTURAL RESOURCES
The project site is not in or adjacent to an agricultural area, therefore, the Lead Agency has determined
no significant impact to agricultural resources is anticipated.
IMPACT ON AESTHETIC RESOURCES
According to the Tompkins County Scenic Resource Views, there are no scenic resources located adjacent
to or in vicinity of the Project Site. Additionally, there are no locally identified scenic resources located
near the project site.
The project includes the removal of a one-story house and the construction of a eight-story apartment
building with a total of 112 beds. The large building will be highly visible on both College Avenue. The
massing of the building is mitigated by contrasting materiality, color choice and breaking the building
along College Avenue into distinct shapes. Site design choices include maintaining streetscape design
along College Avenue.
Based on the information above, the Lead Agency has determined that no significant impacts to
aesthetic resources is anticipated.
IMPACT ON HISTORIC AND ARCHAEOLOGICAL RESOURCES
The site is not located within a historic district, and the existing site is not designated at the local or state
level as a historic resource. However, the project site is located directly South of a building that has been
determined to be eligible for historic designation and in close proximity, within 600’, to the East Hill
Historic District, which is locally and nationally designated. The historic district isnotvisible from the
project site.

City of Ithaca

Based on the information provided above, the Lead Agency has determined no significant impact on
historic and archaeological resources is anticipated.
IMPACT ON OPEN SPACE AND RECREATION
The project site is in a developed mixed-use, residential and commercial neighborhoodconsisting of
mainlymultiple residences often rented by university studentsand commercial buildings. The site isnot
contiguous to any public park or open space. Bryant Park, a publicly ownedpassive recreational facility is
approximately 1100 fteast of the project site. Cascadilla Gorge UNA is also located over 800 ftto the
north of the site.The proposed project will not impact either of the forementioned recreational areas.
As a result of the information provided above, the Lead Agency has determinedthat no significant
impact to open space and recreation is anticipated.
IMPACT ON CRITICAL ENVIRONMENTAL AREAS
There are no critical environmental areas located within the City of Ithaca. However, Tompkins County
identifies Unique Natural Areas (“UNAs”) throughout the county, which are part of the landscape that has
outstanding geological and environmental qualities, such as special natural communities, or plants and
animals that are rare or scarce elsewhere in the county or region.A UNA is not a regulatory designation
and does not provide legal protection for an area butsignals that special resources may exist that require
project modification.
The project site is over 800 ft to the closest UNA, 136- Cascadilla Gorge, a steep forested riparian area
with unique geological features.The project site is located down slope from the UNA, so will have little to
no impact on it.
As a result of the information provided above, the Lead Agency has determined no significant impact to
Critical Environmental Areas is anticipated.
IMPACT ON TRANSPORTATION
Provide Construction Stagingand ParkingPlanand details
Biking spacesall in covered area?
The project site is in an urbanized area in central Collegetown. It is connected to the Citysidewalk system,
is proximate to goods, services and transit and is within walking distance to Cornell University. The project
will also includeretail spacethat encourages active use.
Vehicular Traffic
District regulations in the MU-2 zoning district do not require off-street parking, and the applicant
proposes no parking spaces.
Bicycle & Pedestrian
The applicant proposes a total of 23 bicycle parking spaces in a covered parking shelter located on the
rear of the building.

As a result of the information provided above, the Lead Agency has determined that no significant
impact on transportationis anticipated.
IMPACT ON ENERGY
On August 4, 2021, the Ithaca Energy Code Supplement (IECS) went into effect for all new buildings
constructed in Ithaca. The IECS prioritizes electrification, renewable energy, and affordability with the
following objectives:
“deliver measurable and immediate reductions in greenhouse gas (GHG) emissions from new buildings,
major renovations, and new additions; promote best practices in the design of affordable buildings to
deliver reduced GHG emissions; and provide a rapid but orderly transition to buildings that do not use
fossil fuels for major building energy needs such as space heating and hot water heating, by 2026. For
construction subject to the Ithaca Energy Code Supplement, requirements for reductions in GHGs go
into effect in three steps: 2021, 2023, and 2026.”
From August 4, 2021, until 2023 all new buildings must produce 40% fewer greenhouse gas emissions
than the Energy Conservation Construction Code of New York State requires. Beginning in 2023, the IECS
will increase the requirements of new construction to produce 80% fewer greenhouse gas emissions
than the Energy Conservation Construction Code of New York State requires, and by 2026 all newly
constructed buildings in Ithaca will be required to be net-zero buildings that do not use fossil fuels. The
IECS supports Ithaca’s Green New Deal which aims to “achieve an equitable transition to carbon-
neutrality” community-wide by 2030.
The Building Division will oversee implementation and enforcement of the IECS.
As a result,from the information provided above, the Lead Agency has determined that no significant
impact to energy is anticipated.
IMPACT ON NOISE, ODOR, AND LIGHT

The residential project is not expected to produce operational noise, odor or light that is out of character
with surrounding uses. According to lighting plans titled L-103and , dated February 18, 2026. The exterior
lighting will be dark sky compliant.
According to the FEAF Part 1provided by the applicant, construction will last approximately 21 months.
The project is in a residentialarea. Noise and odors from construction activities could have a significant
but temporary impact the neighborhood. Due to its proximity to neighboring structures, construction
noise will particularly affect adjacent properties.
Noise producing construction activities will temporarily impact residents in the immediate area. Noise
producing construction activities will be limited to the hours between 7:30 A.M. and 5:30 P.M., Monday
through Friday (or Saturday 9:00 A.M. to 5:30 P.M. with advance notification to and approval by the
Director of Planning and Development).
The proposed foundation is cast-in-place concrete. Drilled pile shoring will be required to support
excavations. The applicants will provide documentation from licensed engineers to city staff as to whether
a vibration monitoring plan is a necessity and will provide such plan if it is determined to be necessary.
As a result of this information, the Lead Agency has determined no significant impact on noise, odor, and
light is anticipated.
IMPACT ON HUMAN HEALTH
Provide Phase 1 Environmental Site Assessment
This is a residential project that does not include operational uses that present a threat to human health.
According to aPhase 1 Environmental Site Assessment prepared byQPK Design…
The existing buildingand site featureswill be completely removed and replaced. Demolition may include
removal of toxic substances such as asbestos or lead paint. Removal and disposal of any hazardous
building materials will be done by a licensed contractor and in accordance with state and local Laws.
As a result of this information, the Lead Agency has determined no significant impact to human health is
anticipated.
CONSISTENCY WITH COMMUNITY PLANS
The project is consistent with the City of Ithaca’s Comprehensive Plan, local zoning and its Collegetown
land use category which calls for active streetscapes to enliven the pedestrian experience. The project is
also in line with the economic vitality section of Plan Ithacawhere it states, “Appropriate compact, mixed-

of our established residential neighborhoods.”
The project also meets the purpose, intent and requirements of the MU-2 zoning district along College
Avenue, as outlined in Ithaca’s Collegetown Area Form Districts, which is to concentrate the majority of
additional development and higher density within this district and create dynamic urban environments
that promote a walkable neighborhood.
Based on the information described above, the Lead Agency has determined that no significant impact
to community plans is anticipated.
CONSISTENCY WITH COMMUNITY CHARACTER
The applicant proposes 77residential units with a unit mix ranging from studio to 4-bedroom
apartments for a total of 112beds, 3,250SF of amenity space, and an approximately 1,500SF retail
space on the ground floor. The applicant proposes to maintain the consistency of College Avenue along
the sidewalk including the preservation of an existing tree.
The project is located within both the MU-2 zoning district and will not require area variances. The
project is also subject to Collegetown Design Guidelines.The project is in a Mixed University 2 district,
which is predominantly multiple residences rented by university students and commercial spaces on the
ground floor. The existing sitecontains a one-story fire houseand a parking lot. The Collegetown Core,
as this area where the project site is located is referred in the City of Ithaca’s Collegetown Design
Guidelines adopted in 2018, is densely developed and highly urban in character with mixed-use
buildings with ground floor commercialand residential above and built close to the sidewalk edge.The
proposed building is residential.
The project team presented to the Planning Board at the April 28, 2026meeting mitigations intended to
ensure the building would be consistent with the character of the community and sensitive to the
surrounding context.These mitigations include but are not limited to….
The Planning Board gave the following feedback during design review:
See Consistency with Community Plans.
Based on the information provided above, the Lead Agency has determined no significant impact on
community character is anticipated.

PROPOSED RESOLUTION City of Ithaca Planning & Development Board
Preliminary & Final Approval Site Plan Approval
Finger Lakes ReUse Site Improvements
214 Elmira Road
March24,2026
WHEREAS: the City of Ithaca Planning and Development Board has one pending application for site
plan approval for constructing two awnings and repaving by Diane Cohen, and
WHEREAS: the applicant proposes making site improvements and installing two 600 SF awnings to
the east and west sides of the existing buildings. The project site improvements will disturb
approximately 29,185 SF (.67 acres) of the 2.37-acre site and include removing paving, improving
drainage, formalizing parking and loading areas, and adding green space. The applicants propose
adding 22 parking spaces to the site for a total of 67 proposed spaces. The project is located in the B-5
zoning district and is expected to not require any variances, and
WHEREAS: this is an Unlisted Action under the City of Ithaca Environmental Quality Review
Ordinance §176-4 and the State Environmental Quality Review Act (“SEQRA”) §617.4 and is subject
to environmental review, and
WHEREAS: the City of Ithaca Planning and Development Board, being the local agency, which has
primary responsibility for approving and funding or carrying out the action, did on January 27, 2026,
declare itself Lead Agency in Environmental Review for the project, and
WHEREAS: the Planning Board, acting as Lead Agency in Environmental Review, did on February 24,
2026, review and accept as adequate: a Short Environmental Assessment Form (SEAF), Part 1 prepared
by the applicant; SEAF Part 2 & 3 prepared by Planning staff; Existing Conditions Plan C-100,
Demolition Plan C-101, Site Plan C-102, Circulation and Planting Plan C-103, Photometric Plan C-104,
Erosion & Sediment Control Plan C-105, Site Details C-106 & C-107, all dated 12/12/25 and prepared
by Sciarabba Engineering PLLC; Floor Plan AP1.01, Perspective Views AP1.02, Elevation AP1.04.1,
AP1.04.2 & AP1.05, Illustrated Perspectives (2), all dated February 17, 2026 and prepared by Trade
Design Build; and other application materials, and
WHEREAS: interested parties have been given the opportunity to comment on the proposed project, and
any received comments have been considered, and
WHEREAS: legal notice was published and property posted in accordance with Chapters 276-6 B. (4)
and 176-12 A. (2) (c) of the City of Ithaca Code, and
WHEREAS: the Planning and Development Board held the required public hearing beginning on
February 24, 2026, and
WHEREAS: the Planning Board conducted Design Review for the Project on February 24, 2026, and
found the project to be largely compliant with the Southwest Design Guidelines, and
WHEREAS: the Planning Board did, on February 24, 2026, determine, as elaborated in the FEAF Part 3,
that the proposed project will result in no significant adverse impacts on the environment and issued a
Negative Declaration of Environmental Significance, and
WHEREAS: this Board did on March 24, 2026, review and accept as adequate the following new and
revised drawings: Existing Conditions Plan C-100, Demolition Plan C-101, Site Plan C-102, Circulation

and Planting Plan C-103, Photometric Plan C-104, Erosion & Sediment Control Plan C-105, Site Details
C-106 & C-10 ,all dated 03-13-2026 and prepared by Sciarabba Engineering PLLC, and
WHEREAS: the Board of Zoning Administrator reviewed the site plan changes and determined the
changes are in compliance with the requirements for the B-5 zone, and
WHEREAS: the Planning Board, has determined that the proposed changes are consistent with the
Negative Declaration of Environmental Significance issued by this Board as Lead agency on February
24, 2026 and that further environmental review is not required, now, therefore, be it
RESOLVED: that the Planning and Development Board does hereby grant Preliminary & Final Site Plan
Approval subject to the following:
The following conditions must be satisfied before issuance of a Building Permit:
i. This site plan approval does not preclude any other permit that is required by City Code, such as
sign permits, tree permits, street permits
ii. Documentation from Ithaca Fire Department emergency access issues have been satisfied
The following conditions must be satisfied within six months of approval or the start of construction,
whichever comes first:
i. Submission to the Planning Board for review and approval of all site details including but not limited
to exterior furnishings, walls, railings, bollards, paving, signage, interpretive signage, lighting
showing dark sky compliance), etc.
ii. Any changes to the approved project must be submitted to Planning Staff for review and may
require Board approval
iii. Plans, drawings and/or visualizations showing fence to be installed beneath the western awning
near the building
The following conditions must be satisfied during the construction period:
i. Noise producing construction activities will be limited to the hours between 7:30 A.M. and 5:30
P.M., Monday through Friday (or Saturday 9:00 A.M. to 5:30 P.M. with advance notification to
and approval by the Director of Planning and Development)
ii. If impacted groundwater and/or soil are encountered during construction, it is required to be
handled in accordance with the applicable NYSDEC regulations and requirements and with the
involvement of both the City of Ithaca and the NYSDEC in the approval and monitoring of the
treatment system(s)
The following conditions must be satisfied before issuance of a Certificate of Occupancy:
i. Submission to the Planning Board for review and approval of placement, design, and updated
photometrics of site lighting fixtures
ii. Installation of bike racks and/or bike storage

iii. Installation of any required or proposed sidewalk to the satisfaction of the City Sidewalk
Coordinator
iv. Repair, replacement or reconstruction of any City property damaged or removed during
construction including, but not limited to paving, sidewalk, curbing, trees or tree lawn, signage,
drainage structures, etc.

WARNING:
It is a violation of Section 7209, Subdivision 2 of the New
York State Education Law for any person, unless he or she
is working under the direction of a licensed engineer, to
alter an item in any way. If an item bearing the seal of an
engineer is altered, the altering engineer shall affix to the
item his or her seal and the notation "altered by" followed
by his or her signature, the date of such alteration, and a
specific description of the alteration.
